# Why: the EPYC's files of log1p (the latest log1p-epyc7763-2026-11-21.txt, run 44923942033, main at
# 9b9cc7b) all time code from before #100, and issue #30's rules wait for current files of each
# host. bench/record.py would not join this run to that file, since the code under Log1pBenchmark
# differs between 9b9cc7b and 7413a1d in more than comments (below), so it is a first run of new
# code. Run 2 below is its re-run, dispatched the same way on main at 18b9a9a: run 45199592885, 23
# November 2026, from 14:56 UTC. This file holds Log1pBenchmark's blocks and rows of both runs.
# bench/record.py write made it from the two runs' artifacts, and bench/record.py check

# from "Host" to "Command" are the workflow's header; "Array" describes the benchmark; each
# min/avg/max table is taken from its run's JMH "Result" blocks in the raw output.
#
# The code: between 9b9cc7b and 7413a1d, the one file under Log1pBenchmark that bench/record.py
# finds different in more than comments is Log1p.java, which took #100 (5a452fa), the
# allocation-free SPECIES_MAX finish: the finishMax rows of both runs time the finish that writes
# out its first eight lanes with constant indices, where the 21 November file's boxed on mixed data.
# The other commits that touched Log1p.java changed comments only, and Log1pBenchmark.java and
# pom.xml are the same but for comments. The JMH options include -foe true, so a row that failed
# would have failed the run; every row of Log1pBenchmark has a Result block, which bench/record.py
# requires.
#
# The re-run's code: 18b9a9a is not 7413a1d, and bench/record.py joins the two runs in one file
